Thanks: Jehan Oh is supported by the National Research Foundation of Korea (NRF) grant funded by the Korea government [Grant Nos. RS-2023-00217116, RS-2025-00555316, and RS-2025-25415411]. Abhrojyoti Sen is supported by postdoctoral research grants from the Alexander von Humboldt Foundation, Germany.


Abstract
. We establish an interior gradient higher integrability result for weak solutions to degenerate parabolic double phase systems involving two modulating coefficients. To be more precise, we study systems of the form u t − div ⁡ ( a ⁡ ( z ) ​ | D ​ u | p − 2 ​ D ​ u + b ⁡ ( z ) ​ | D ​ u | q − 2 ​ D ​ u ) = − div ⁡ ( a ⁡ ( z ) ​ | F | p − 2 ​ F + b ⁡ ( z ) ​ | F | q − 2 ​ F ) , \displaystyle u_{t}-\dv\left(a(z)|Du|^{p-2}Du+b(z)|Du|^{q-2}Du\right)=-\dv\left(a(z)|F|^{p-2}F+b(z)|F|^{q-2}F\right), where 2 ≤ p ≤ q < ∞ 2\leq p\leq q<\infty and the modulating coefficients a ⁡ ( z ) a(z) and b ⁡ ( z ) b(z) are non-negative, with a ⁡ ( z ) a(z) being uniformly continuous and b ⁡ ( z ) b(z) being Hölder continuous. We further assume that the sum of two modulating coefficients is bounded from below by some positive constant. To establish the gradient higher integrability result, we introduce a suitable intrinsic geometry and develop a delicate comparison scheme to separate and analyze the different phases–namely, the p p -phase, q q -phase and ( p , q ) (p,q) -phase. To the best of our knowledge, this is the first regularity result in the parabolic setting that addresses general double phase systems within the framework of weak solutions.



Gradient higher integrability for degenerate parabolic double phase systems with two modulating coefficients

Jehan Oh
1
1

1

jehan.oh@knu.ac.kr
and Abhrojyoti Sen

2

sen@math.uni-frankfurt.de

Department of Mathematics, Kyungpook National University, Daegu, 41566,

Republic of Korea

Goethe-Universität Frankfurt, Institut für Mathematik, Robert-Mayer-Str. 10,

D-60629 Frankfurt, Germany

35B65, 35D30, 35K40, 35K55, 35K65.

1.
Introduction and main result

In this paper, we study the higher integrability of the spatial gradient of a weak solution to degenerate parabolic double phase systems of the form

(1.1)

u_{t}-\operatorname*{div}\mathcal{A}(z,u,Du)=-\operatorname*{div}\mathcal{B}(z,F)\quad\text{in $\Omega_{T}$,}

where
\Omega_{T}:=\Omega\times(0,T)
with a bounded open set
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
for
n\geq 2
.
Here, the Carathéodory vector fields
{\mathcal{A}}={\mathcal{A}}(z,v,\xi):\Omega_{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{N}\times\mathbb{R}^{Nn}\to\mathbb{R}^{Nn}
and
\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{B}(z,\xi):\Omega_{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{Nn}\to\mathbb{R}^{Nn}
with
N\geq 1
satisfy the following structural assumptions: there exist two constants
0<\nu\leq L
such that for any
z\in\Omega_{T},\;v\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{Nn}
,

(1.2)

\nu H(z,|\xi|)\leq\mathcal{A}(z,v,\xi)\cdot\xi,\qquad|\mathcal{A}(z,v,\xi)||\xi|\leq LH(z,|\xi|)

and

(1.3)

|\mathcal{B}(z,\xi)||\xi|\leq LH(z,|\xi|),

where the function
H:\Omega_{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
is defined by

H(z,\kappa)=a(z)\kappa^{p}+b(z)\kappa^{q}

for
z\in\Omega_{T}
and
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
In addition, the source term
F:\Omega_{T}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{Nn}
satisfies

(1.4)

{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int_{\Omega_{T}}}H(z,|F(z)|)\,dz<+\infty.

When
a(\cdot)\equiv 1
and
b(\cdot)\equiv 0
, the system reduces to the classical parabolic
p
-Laplace one. In this case, a comprehensive regularity theory, including higher integrability and Hölder continuity, is well-known, see
[
12
,
14
,
13
,
28
,
15
,
4
]
and the references given there.

When
a(\cdot)\equiv 1
and
b(\cdot)\not\equiv 0
, it reduces to the parabolic double phase system with a single modulating coefficient. This classical model, originally introduced by Zhikov for the elliptic case
[
31
,
32
,
33
,
34
]
, has been widely used in the study of double phase problems, which arise in the modeling of strongly anisotropic materials. Notably, this model also serves as a prototypical example exhibiting the Lavrentiev phenomenon, as discussed in
[
16
,
17
,
32
,
33
]
. Such problems have been extensively studied and continue to attract attention. In the elliptic setting, a broad range of regularity results, including
C^{\alpha}/C^{1,\alpha}
-estimates, Calderón-Zygmund estimates and Harnack inequality, have been established in the pioneering works
[
2
,
9
,
10
,
8
,
11
,
1
,
6
,
5
]
. On the other hand, in the parabolic setting, results such as gradient higher integrability
[
22
,
25
]
, Calderón–Zygmund estimates
[
26
,
27
]
, existence and uniqueness of solutions
[
30
,
7
]
, and Hölder continuity
[
24
]
have been obtained only recently. However, the classical model involves only one modulating coefficient, which is limited in describing materials composed of both
p
-hardening and
q
-hardening components. Although the case involving two modulating coefficients is mathematically more demanding, it provides a more suitable framework for modeling strongly anisotropic composites. For the double phase problems with two modulating coefficients, the elliptic case has been studied in recent works such as
[
20
,
3
,
21
]
, but to the best of our knowledge, there are no results for the parabolic case.
This paper addresses the parabolic double phase problem with two modulating coefficients, aiming to prove the regularity of solutions by introducing an appropriate intrinsic geometry and developing a delicate phase separation.

To avoid the simultaneous vanishing of the two modulating coefficients, we assume that their sum is bounded from below by a positive constant, that is,

(1.5)

a(z)+b(z)\geq\delta,\quad\forall z\in\Omega_{T}

for some
\delta>0
.
As in the classical model, we assume that the modulating coefficient
b:\Omega_{T}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
is in
C^{\alpha,\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\Omega_{T})
for some
\alpha\in(0,1]
and we impose the restriction

(1.6)

2\leq p\leq q\leq p+\frac{2\alpha}{n+2}<\infty.

In particular, the assumption
b\in C^{\alpha,\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\Omega_{T})
implies that
b\in L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})
and there exists a constant
[b]_{\alpha}>0
such that

|b(x_{1},t_{1})-b(x_{2},t_{2})|\leq[b]_{\alpha}\left(|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha}+|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\right)

for any
(x_{1},t_{1}),(x_{2},t_{2})\in\Omega_{T}
.

We also assume that the modulating coefficient
a:\Omega_{T}\to\mathbb{R}^{+}
is bounded and uniformly continuous, that is, there exists a modulus of continuity
\omega_{a}
such that

(1.7)

|a(z_{1})-a(z_{2})|\leq\omega_{a}(d(z_{1},z_{2}))

for any
z_{1},z_{2}\in\Omega_{T}
, where
d
is the parabolic metric defined in
Definition

2.1
.

To state the main theorem, we first introduce the definition of a weak solution to
Eq.

1.1
.

Definition 1.1
.

A function
u:\Omega\times(0,T]\to\mathbb{R}^{N}
with

\displaystyle u\in C_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(0,T;L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{N}\right)\right)\cap L^{q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(0,T;W^{1,q}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{N}\right)\right)

is said to be a weak solution to (
1.1
) if for every compact subset
K\subset\Omega
and for every subinterval
[t_{1},t_{2}]\subset(0,T]
, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{K}-u\cdot\varphi_{t}\,dx\,dt+\int_{K}u\cdot\varphi\,dx\Big|_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}+\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{K}{\mathcal{A}}(z,u,Du)\cdot D\varphi\,dx\,dt=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\int_{K}\mathcal{B}(z,F)\cdot D\varphi\,dx\,dt

for any test function
\varphi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(K\times(0,T],\mathbb{R}^{N}).

We now state the main theorem of the paper.

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
u
be a weak solution of (
1.1
) with (
1.2
)–(
1.7
) in force. Then there exist positive constants
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(\textnormal{{data}})\in(0,1)
and
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})\geq 1
such that the following estimate

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{r}(z_{0})}}H_{1}(z,|Du|)^{1+\varepsilon}\,dz\leq c\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2r}(z_{0})}}H_{1}(z,|Du|)\,dz\right)^{1+\frac{\varepsilon q}{2}}+c\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2r}(z_{0})}}H_{1}(z,|F|)^{1+\varepsilon}\,dz\right)^{\frac{q}{2}}

holds for every
Q_{2r}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}
and any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0})
, where
Q_{(\cdot)}(z_{0})
,
H_{1}(z,\cdot)
and
data
are defined in Subsection
2.1

(v)
,
(vi)
and
(viii)
, respectively.

We make a couple of remarks on the result above.

Remark 1.1
.

It should be noted that the above theorem is stated under the technical assumption
|Du|\in L^{q}(\Omega_{T}).
However, a more natural assumption would be to consider

\displaystyle u\in C_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(0,T;L^{2}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{N}\right)\right)\cap L^{1}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(0,T;W^{1,1}_{\mathrm{loc}}\left(\Omega,\mathbb{R}^{N}\right)\right)

along with the integrability condition

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{T}}H(z,|Du|)\,dz=\iint_{\Omega_{T}}\left[a(z)|Du|^{p}+b(z)|Du|^{q}\right]dz<\infty.

A parabolic Lipschitz truncation method (see
[
23
]
for
a(\cdot)\equiv 1
) can be used to obtain the energy estimate under this assumption. This will be pursued in future work.

Remark 1.2
.

This paper focuses on the degenerate regime
2\leq p\leq q<\infty
. Extending the analysis to the singular regime
\frac{2n}{n+2}<p<2
would be an interesting direction. Recently, in
[
29
]
, a unified scaling approach was introduced to establish gradient higher integrability for degenerate and singular parabolic multi-phase problems. Applying such an intrinsic scaling technique in our setting would be an important avenue for further exploration.

We close this discussion by highlighting some of the novelties and strategies to prove
Theorem

1.1
.

(i)   A commonly used strategy for establishing higher integrability of gradients in parabolic equations typically involves two main steps: first, proving reverse Hölder-type inequalities on intrinsic cylinders, and second, demonstrating the existence of such cylinders within certain superlevel sets. The final proof follows from a Vitali covering argument combined with Fubini’s theorem.

Although our approach to proving
Theorem

1.1
aligns broadly with
[
22
]
, we introduce a refined comparison scheme to appropriately distinguish different phases. The presence of the coefficient
a(\cdot)
and the parabolic nature of the system (
1.1
) introduce additional intricacy, setting our approach apart from
[
22
]
and
[
20
]
. Specifically, depending on the smallness of
a(\cdot)
and
b(\cdot)
, we classify the behavior into distinct phases: the
p
-phase, the
q
-phase, and the mixed
(p,q)
-phase, which is one of the main novelties of this paper.
We establish reverse Hölder inequalities for each phase and implement a Vitali covering argument that addresses nine distinct cases.

(ii)   Now, we describe our phase separation procedure and compare it with that of parabolic double phase systems with a single modulating coefficient, i.e.,
a(\cdot)\equiv 1
. First, consider the elliptic
p
-phase, characterized by
b(z_{0})\leq[b]_{\alpha}\tau^{\alpha}.
In this case, two possibilities arise: either
K\lambda^{p}\geq b(z_{0})\lambda^{q}
or
K\lambda^{p}\leq b(z_{0})\lambda^{q}.
It is shown in
[
22
]
that the conditions
b(z_{0})\leq[b]_{\alpha}\tau^{\alpha}
and
K\lambda^{p}\leq b(z_{0})\lambda^{q}
cannot simultaneously hold. Moreover, the following assumption

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ^{\lambda}_{\tau}(z_{0})}}\left[H(z,|Du|)+H(z,|F|)\right]dz<\lambda^{p}

is imposed to characterize the
p
-phase.
Next, we consider the elliptic
(p,q)
-phase, where
b(z_{0})\geq[b]_{\alpha}\tau^{\alpha}.
Again, two cases emerge: either
K\lambda^{p}\geq b(z_{0})\lambda^{q}
or
K\lambda^{p}\leq b(z_{0})\lambda^{q}.
Additionally, the assumption in
[
22
]
for
(p,q)
-phase includes

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptG^{\lambda}_{\tau}(z_{0})}}\left[H(z,|Du|)+H(z,|F|)\right]dz<\lambda^{p}+b(z_{0})\lambda^{q}.

Note that, if
K\lambda^{p}\geq b(z_{0})\lambda^{q}
then the above condition essentially leads to the analysis of
p
-intrinsic case, reducing the scope of nontrivial distinct phases to the case
K\lambda^{p}\leq b(z_{0})\lambda^{q}.

In contrast, our setting is more intricate due to the presence of the coefficient
a(\cdot),
leading to eight distinct possibilities depending on whether
a(z_{0})\geq\omega_{a}(\tau)
or
a(z_{0})\leq\omega_{a}(\tau),
as detailed in
Section

4
. These distinctions result in three separate cases, unlike the framework in
[
22
]
, as described in (
3.1
), (
3.6
), and (
3.10
)-(
3.11
). Depending on the size of the coefficients
a(\cdot)
and
b(\cdot)
in the respective intrinsic cylinders, the system (
1.1
) can behave as a
p
-system, or a
q
-system or a
(p,q)
-system. Moreover, note that we only need a uniform continuity of
a(\cdot).

The remaining part of the paper is organized as follows.
In
Section

2
, we set our notations, record some known results and prove the energy estimate.

Section

3
is dedicated to proving the reverse Hölder inequalities for the
p
-,
q
-, and
(p,q)
-phases.
In
Section

4
, we establish our gradient higher integrability result stated in
Theorem

1.1
. The proof begins with a stopping time argument, followed by the use of the Vitali covering lemma. Finally, we employ Fubini’s theorem together with reverse Hölder inequalities to conclude the result.

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
Notations

Here we collect the standard notation that will be used throughout the paper:

(i)

We shall denote by
n
the space dimension and by
z=(x,t)
a point in
{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\times(0,T)
. We will always assume that
n\geq 2
.

(ii)

A ball with center
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and radius
r
is denoted as

\displaystyle B_{r}(x_{0})=\left\{x\in{\mathbb{R}^{n}}:|x-x_{0}|<r\right\}.

(iii)

In general, a parabolic cylinder centered at
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0})
is denoted as

\displaystyle U_{r,\varrho}(z_{0}):=B_{r}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\varrho,t_{0}+\varrho\right)=:B_{r}(x_{0})\times l_{\varrho}(t_{0}).

(iv)

Let
\lambda\geq 1.
We shall use the following three types of cylinders for the
p
-,
q
-, and
(p,q)
-phases.

The notation
Q_{\varrho,\lambda}(z_{0})
is used to denote
p
-intrinsic cylinders at
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0}),

\displaystyle Q_{\varrho,\lambda}(z_{0}):=B_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\frac{\lambda^{2-p}\varrho^{2}}{a(z_{0})},t_{0}+\frac{\lambda^{2-p}\varrho^{2}}{a(z_{0})}\right)=:B_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times I_{\varrho,\lambda}(t_{0}).

The notation
J_{\varrho,\lambda}(z_{0})
is used to denote
q
-intrinsic cylinders at
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0}),

\displaystyle J_{\varrho,\lambda}(z_{0}):=B_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\frac{\lambda^{2-q}\varrho^{2}}{b(z_{0})},t_{0}+\frac{\lambda^{2-q}\varrho^{2}}{b(z_{0})}\right)=:B_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times I^{q}_{\varrho,\lambda}(t_{0}).

The notation
G_{\varrho,\lambda}(z_{0})
is used to denote
(p,q)
-intrinsic cylinders at
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0}),

\displaystyle G_{\varrho,\lambda}(z_{0}):=B_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times\left(t_{0}-\frac{\lambda^{2}\varrho^{2}}{\Lambda},t_{0}+\frac{\lambda^{2}\varrho^{2}}{\Lambda}\right)=:B_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times I^{(p,q)}_{\varrho,\lambda}(t_{0}),

where
\Lambda:=a(z_{0})\lambda^{p}+b(z_{0})\lambda^{q}.

(v)

In the case
\lambda=1
and
a(z_{0})=1,
we denote

Q_{\varrho}(z_{0}):=B_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times(t_{0}-\varrho^{2},t_{0}+\varrho^{2})=:B_{\varrho}(x_{0})\times I_{\varrho}(t_{0}).

(vi)

We shall use the notation
H_{1}(z,\kappa):=a(z)\kappa^{p}+b(z)\kappa^{q}+1
for
z\in\Omega_{T}
and
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

(vii)

Integration with respect to either space or time only will be denoted by a single integral
\int
whereas integration on
\Omega\times(0,T)
will be denoted by a double integral
\iint
.

(viii)

The notation
a\lesssim b
is shorthand for
a\leq cb
where
c
is a universal constant which depends on
``\text{{data}}"
where

\displaystyle\text{{data}}:=\left(n,N,p,q,\alpha,\nu,L,\delta,\omega_{a},[b]_{\alpha},||a||_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})},||b||_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})},||H(z,|Du|)||_{L^{1}(\Omega_{T})},||H(z,|F|)||_{L^{1}(\Omega_{T})}\right).

(ix)

We denote

\displaystyle M:=\iint_{\Omega_{T}}\left[H(z,|Du|)+H(z,|F|)\right]dz<\infty.

(x)

We shall denote

\displaystyle(f)_{\mathcal{D}}:=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}|}\iint_{\mathcal{D}}f(z)\,dz={{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899pt\mathcal{D}}}f(z)\,dz

as the integral average of
f
over a measurable set
\mathcal{D}\subset\Omega_{T}
with
0<|\mathcal{D}|<\infty.
In particular, when
\mathcal{D}=Q_{\varrho,\lambda}(z_{0}),J_{\varrho,\lambda}(z_{0}),G_{\varrho,\lambda}(z_{0}),
the integral average of
f
is denoted by
(f)_{z_{0},\varrho,\lambda},(f)^{q}_{z_{0},\varrho,\lambda},(f)^{(p,q)}_{z_{0},\varrho,\lambda},
respectively.

We also introduce the definition of the parabolic metric and its scaled version.

Definition 2.1

(Parabolic metric)
.

Given any two points
z_{1}=(x_{1},t_{1})
and
z_{2}=(x_{2},t_{2})
in
\mathbb{R}^{n+1},
we define the parabolic metric
d
as

\displaystyle d(z_{1},z_{2}):=\max\left\{|x_{1}-x_{2}|,\sqrt{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\right\}.

A suitable adaptation of the above definition in intrinsically scaled cylinders centered at
z_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
is as follows.

Definition 2.2

(Scaled parabolic metric)
.

Fix
z_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}.
Given any two points
z_{1}=(x_{1},t_{1}),z_{2}=(x_{2},t_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{n+1}
and
\lambda\geq 1
, we define the parabolic metric
d^{\lambda}_{z_{0}}
as

\displaystyle\displaystyle d^{\lambda}_{z_{0}}(z_{1},z_{2}):=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\max\left\{|x_{1}-x_{2}|,\sqrt{a(z_{0})\lambda^{p-2}|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\right\},\quad\text{in $p$-intrinsic case,}\\
\max\left\{|x_{1}-x_{2}|,\sqrt{b(z_{0})\lambda^{q-2}|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\right\},\quad\text{in $q$-intrinsic case,}\\
\max\left\{|x_{1}-x_{2}|,\sqrt{\Lambda\lambda^{-2}|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\right\},\quad\quad\,\,\,\,\,\text{in $(p,q)$-intrinsic case,}\end{array}\right.

where
\Lambda:=a(z_{0})\lambda^{p}+b(z_{0})\lambda^{q}.

2.2.
Basic results and useful lemmas

In this subsection, we introduce several lemmas that will be used later.
We begin by recalling the notations
(iii)
,
(v)
and
(x)
. Parabolic cylinders

U_{r,\tau}(z_{0})
and
Q_{r}(z_{0})
are defined by

U_{r,\tau}(z_{0}):=B_{r}(x_{0})\times(t_{0}-\tau,t_{0}+\tau),\qquad Q_{r}(z_{0}):=B_{r}(x_{0})\times(t_{0}-r^{2},t_{0}+r^{2})

for
z_{0}=(x_{0},t_{0})
, and the average integral of
u
over
U_{r,\tau}(z_{0})
is defined as

(u)_{U_{r,\tau}(z_{0})}:={{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{r,\tau}(z_{0})}}u(z)\,dz.

Furthermore, we recall that

\displaystyle H(z,|Du|)=a(z)|Du|^{p}+b(z)|Du|^{q}.

The following lemma presents a Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality, which will play a key role in establishing reverse Hölder’s inequalities.

Lemma 2.1

(
[
19
]
, Lemma 2.12)
.

For an open ball
B_{\rho}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
, let
\psi\in W^{1,s}(B_{\rho})
and take
\sigma,s,r\in[1,\infty)
and
\vartheta\in(0,1)
such that

-\frac{n}{\sigma}\leq\vartheta\left(1-\frac{n}{s}\right)-(1-\vartheta)\frac{n}{r}.

Then we have

\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho}}\frac{|\psi|^{\sigma}}{\rho^{\sigma}}\,dx\leq c\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho}}\left(\frac{|\psi|^{s}}{\rho^{s}}+|D\psi|^{s}\right)dx\right)^{\frac{\vartheta\sigma}{s}}\left(\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{\rho}}\frac{|\psi|^{r}}{\rho^{r}}dx\right)^{\frac{(1-\vartheta)\sigma}{r}}

for some constant
c=c(n,\sigma)
.

We next record a simple but fundamental iteration lemma from
[
18
, Lemma 1.1]
:

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
0<r<R<\infty
be given and
h:[r,R]\to\mathbb{R}
be a non-negative bounded function. Furthermore, let
\vartheta\in(0,1)
and
A,B,\gamma,\geq 0
be fixed constants and
suppose that

h(\varrho_{1})\leq\vartheta h(\varrho_{2})+\frac{A}{(\varrho_{2}-\varrho_{1})^{\gamma}}+B,

holds for all
r\leq\varrho_{1}<\varrho_{2}\leq R
.
Then the following conclusion holds:

h(r)\lesssim_{(\vartheta,\gamma)}\frac{A}{(R-r)^{\gamma}}+B.

Now we recall the notation
(vi)
from Subsection
2.1
. We define
H_{1}:\Omega_{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
by

H_{1}(z,\kappa):=H(z,\kappa)+1=a(z)\kappa^{p}+b(z)\kappa^{q}+1

for
z\in\Omega_{T}
and
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.
In this paper, we use
H_{1}
instead of
H
for technical convenience as in the following lemma to establish the key inequalities and estimates necessary to prove the main result.

Lemma 2.3
.

There exist positive constants
c_{1}=c_{1}(\delta)
and
c_{2}=c_{2}(\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})},\,\|b\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})})
such that

c_{1}|\xi|^{p}\leq H_{1}(z,|\xi|)\leq c_{2}(|\xi|^{q}+1)

for all
z\in\Omega_{T}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Proof.

Let
z\in\Omega_{T}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
When
|\xi|\geq 1
, we observe from (
1.5
) that

\displaystyle\delta|\xi|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq(a(z)+b(z))|\xi|^{p}

\displaystyle\leq a(z)|\xi|^{p}+b(z)|\xi|^{q}+1=H_{1}(z,|\xi|)

\displaystyle\leq(a(z)+b(z)+1)|\xi|^{q}

\displaystyle\leq(\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})}+\|b\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})}+1)|\xi|^{q}.

When
|\xi|\leq 1
, we get

\displaystyle|\xi|^{p}\leq 1

\displaystyle\leq a(z)|\xi|^{p}+b(z)|\xi|^{q}+1=H_{1}(z,|\xi|)

\displaystyle\leq a(z)+b(z)+1

\displaystyle\leq\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})}+\|b\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})}+1.

Combining these inequalities, we conclude that for any
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

\min\{\delta,1\}|\xi|^{p}\leq H_{1}(z,|\xi|)\leq(\|a\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})}+\|b\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})}+1)(|\xi|^{q}+1),

and the lemma follows.
∎

We now establish the following Caccioppoli inequality.

Lemma 2.4
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
). Then, for
U_{R,S}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}
,
r\in[R/2,R)
and
\tau\in[S/2^{2},S)
, there exists a constant
c
depending on
n,p,q,\nu
and
L
such that the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(t_{0}-\tau,t_{0}+\tau)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{r,\tau}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{\tau}\;dx+{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{r,\tau}(z_{0})}}H(z,|Du|)\;dz

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}H\left(z,\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|}{R-r}\right)dz

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{S-\tau}\;dz+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}H(z,|F|)\;dz.

Proof.

Consider a cut-off function
\eta\in C^{\infty}_{0}(B_{R}(x_{0}))
satisfying

0\leq\eta\leq 1,\quad\eta\equiv 1\ \text{ in }B_{r}(x_{0}),\quad\text{and}\quad\|D\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{2}{R-r}.

For
\tau\in[S/2^{2},S)
, we choose a sufficiently small
h_{0}>0
such that there exists a cut-off function
\zeta\in C^{\infty}_{0}(I_{S-h_{0}}(t_{0}))
satisfying

0\leq\zeta\leq 1,\quad\zeta\equiv 1\ \text{ in }I_{\tau}(t_{0}),\quad\text{and}\quad\|\partial_{t}\zeta\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\frac{3}{S-\tau}.

Additionally, select
t_{*}\in I_{\tau}(t_{0})
and
\upepsilon\in(0,h_{0})
. Define
\zeta_{\upepsilon}
as follows:

\zeta_{\upepsilon}(t):=\begin{cases}1,&\quad t\in(-\infty,t_{*}-\upepsilon),\\
1-\frac{t-t_{*}+\upepsilon}{\upepsilon},&\quad t\in[t_{*}-\upepsilon,t_{*}],\\
0,&\quad t\in(t_{*},\infty).\end{cases}

Fix
h\in(0,h_{0})
. We express (
1.1
) using Steklov averages as

(2.1)

\partial_{t}[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}-\operatorname*{div}[\mathcal{A}(z,u,Du)]_{h}=-\operatorname*{div}[\mathcal{B}(z,F)]_{h}

in
B_{R}(x_{0})\times I_{S-h}(t_{0})
. Then the function
[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}\eta^{q}\zeta^{2}\zeta_{\upepsilon}
belongs to

W^{1,2}_{0}(I_{S-h};L^{2}(B_{R}(x_{0})))\cap L^{q}(I_{S-h};W^{1,q}_{0}(B_{R}(x_{0}))).

By using
\varphi=[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}\eta^{q}\zeta^{2}\zeta_{\upepsilon}
as a test function in (
2.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle:={{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}\partial_{t}[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}\;\varphi\;dz+{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}[\mathcal{A}(z,u,Du)]_{h}\cdot D\varphi\;dz

\displaystyle={{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}[\mathcal{B}(z,F)]_{h}\cdot D\varphi\;dz=:\mathrm{III}.

We deduce from
|Du|\in L^{q}(\Omega_{T})
and (
1.4
) that
\mathrm{II}
and
\mathrm{III}
are finite. Indeed,

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle\leq L{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}(a|Du|^{p-1})_{h}(x,t)|D\varphi(x,t)|\,dx\,dt

(2.2)

\displaystyle\qquad+L{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}(b|Du|^{q-1})_{h}(x,t)|D\varphi(x,t)|\,dx\,dt.

We will estimate the first term on the right-hand side and the estimation of the second term follows similarly. Using the definition of Steklov averages, the first term can be written as

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}(a|Du|^{p-1})_{h}(x,t)|D\varphi(x,t)|\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle={{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{t}^{t+h}(a(x,s))^{\frac{p-1}{p}}|Du(x,s)|^{p-1}(a(x,s))^{\frac{1}{p}}|D\varphi(x,t)|\,ds\,dx\,dt.

Now using Hölder’s inequality and properties of Steklov averages, we get

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}|(a|Du|^{p-1})_{h}(x,t)||D\varphi(x,t)|\,dx\,dt

\displaystyle\leq c\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}a(x,t)|Du(x,t)|^{p}\,dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{t}^{t+h}a(x,s)|D\varphi(x,t)|^{p}\,ds\,dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=c\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}a(x,t)|Du(x,t)|^{p}\,dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}a_{h}(x,t)|D\varphi(x,t)|^{p}\,dx\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

Using
||a||_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})}<\infty
and
|Du|\in L^{q}(\Omega_{T}),
we conclude that the above inequality is finite. By the same argument, we see that the second term of (
2.2
) is also finite. We then estimate each term separately as follows. Note that estimation of the term
\mathrm{I}
goes same as
[
22
, Lemma 2.3]
. We repeat it here for the sake of completeness.

Estimate of
\mathrm{I}
:
Integrating by parts, we find

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle={{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{t}|[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}|^{2})\eta^{q}\zeta^{2}\zeta_{\upepsilon}\;dz

(2.3)

\displaystyle=-{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}|[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}|^{2}\eta^{q}\zeta\zeta_{\upepsilon}\partial_{t}\zeta\;dz

\displaystyle\qquad-{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}\frac{1}{2}|[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}|^{2}\eta^{q}\zeta^{2}\partial_{t}\zeta_{\upepsilon}\;dz.

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
2.3
), it follows from the definition of
\zeta
that

-{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}|[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}|^{2}\eta^{q}\zeta\zeta_{\upepsilon}\partial_{t}\zeta\;dz\geq-3{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}\frac{|[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}|^{2}}{S-\tau}\;dz.

Next, by the definition of
\zeta_{\upepsilon}
, we get

\displaystyle-{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}\frac{1}{2}|[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}|^{2}\eta^{q}\zeta^{2}\partial_{t}\zeta_{\upepsilon}\;dz

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{|U_{R,S}|}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{t_{*}-\upepsilon}^{t_{*}}\int_{B_{R}(x_{0})}\frac{1}{2}|[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}|^{2}\eta^{q}\zeta^{2}\;dxdt

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{1}{|U_{R,S}|}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{t_{*}-\upepsilon}^{t_{*}}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{1}{2}|[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}|^{2}\;dxdt.

Thus, we obtain

(2.4)

\displaystyle\lim_{h\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim_{\upepsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle\geq-3{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{S-\tau}\;dz

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2|U_{R,S}|}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}|u(x,t_{*})-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|^{2}\;dx.

Estimate of
\mathrm{II}
:
It follows from the definition of
\varphi
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle={{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}[\mathcal{A}(z,u,Du)]_{h}\cdot[Du]_{h}\eta^{q}\zeta^{2}\zeta_{\upepsilon}\;dz

(2.5)

\displaystyle\qquad+q{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}[\mathcal{A}(z,u,Du)]_{h}\cdot[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}D\eta\eta^{q-1}\zeta^{2}\zeta_{\upepsilon}\;dz.

For the first term of (
2.5
), we deduce from (
1.2
) that

\displaystyle\lim_{h\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim_{\upepsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}[\mathcal{A}(z,u,Du)]_{h}\cdot[Du]_{h}\eta^{q}\zeta^{2}\zeta_{\upepsilon}\;dz

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\nu}{|U_{R,S}|}\int_{I_{S}(t_{0})\cap(-\infty,t_{*})}\int_{B_{R}(x_{0})}H(z,|Du|)\eta^{q}\zeta^{2}\;dxdt.

Next, to estimate the second term in (
2.5
), we apply (
1.2
) and the definition of
\eta
to find that

\displaystyle\lim_{h\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim_{\upepsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}q{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}[\mathcal{A}(z,u,Du)]_{h}\cdot[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}D\eta\eta^{q-1}\zeta^{2}\zeta_{\upepsilon}\;dz

\displaystyle\quad\geq-\frac{Lq}{|U_{R,S}|}\int_{I_{S}(t_{0})\cap(-\infty,t_{*})}\int_{B_{R}(x_{0})}a(z)|Du|^{p-1}\eta^{q-1}\zeta^{2}\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|}{R-r}\;dxdt

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{Lq}{|U_{R,S}|}\int_{I_{S}(t_{0})\cap(-\infty,t_{*})}\int_{B_{R}(x_{0})}b(z)|Du|^{q-1}\eta^{q-1}\zeta^{2}\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|}{R-r}\;dxdt.

Young’s inequality with
\varepsilon=\frac{\nu}{8Ls}
implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{h\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim_{\upepsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}q{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}[\mathcal{A}(z,u,Du)]_{h}\cdot[u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}]_{h}D\eta\eta^{q-1}\zeta^{2}\zeta_{\upepsilon}\;dz

\displaystyle\quad\geq-\frac{\nu}{4|U_{R,S}|}\int_{I_{S}(t_{0})\cap(-\infty,t_{*})}\int_{B_{R}(x_{0})}H(z,|Du|)\eta^{q}\zeta^{2}\;dxdt

\displaystyle\quad\qquad-c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}H\left(z,\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|}{R-r}\right)dz

for some constant
c>0
depending on
p,q,\nu
and
L
, since
0\leq\eta\leq 1
and
2\leq p\leq q<\infty
.
It follows that

(2.6)

\displaystyle\lim_{h\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim_{\upepsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{3\nu}{4|U_{R,S}|}\int_{I_{S}(t_{0})\cap(-\infty,t_{*})}\int_{B_{R}(x_{0})}H(z,|Du|)\eta^{q}\zeta^{2}\;dxdt

\displaystyle-c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}H\left(z,\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|}{R-r}\right)dz.

Estimate of
\mathrm{III}
:
As above, using the growth condition (
1.3
), we obtain from Young’s inequality that

\displaystyle\lim_{h\rightarrow 0^{+}}\lim_{\upepsilon\rightarrow 0^{+}}\mathrm{III}

\displaystyle\leq{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}H(z,|F|)\;dz

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\nu}{2|U_{R,S}|}\int_{I_{S}(t_{0})\cap(-\infty,t_{*})}\int_{B_{R}(x_{0})}H(z,|Du|)\eta^{q}\zeta^{2}\;dxdt

(2.7)

\displaystyle\quad+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}H\left(z,\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|}{R-r}\right)dz.

Combining (
2.4
), (
2.6
) and (
2.7
) gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|U_{R,S}|}\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}|u(x,t_{*})-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|^{2}\;dx

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{|U_{R,S}|}\int_{I_{S}(t_{0})\cap(-\infty,t_{*})}\int_{B_{R}(x_{0})}H(z,|Du|)\eta^{q}\zeta^{2}\;dxdt

\displaystyle\quad\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}H\left(z,\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|}{R-r}\right)dz

\displaystyle\qquad+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{S-\tau}\;dz+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}H(z,|F|)\;dz,

where
c
depends only on
p,q,\nu
and
L
. As
t_{*}\in I_{\tau}(t_{0})
is arbitrary,
\frac{R}{2}\leq r<R
and
\frac{S}{4}\leq\tau<S
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in(t_{0}-\tau,t_{0}+\tau)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{\tau}\;dx+{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{r,\tau}(z_{0})}}H(z,|Du|)\;dz

\displaystyle\qquad\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}H\left(z,\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|}{R-r}\right)\;dz

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{S-\tau}\;dz+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}H(z,|F|)\;dz

for some constant
c>0
depending on
n,p,q,\nu
and
L
. Note that

\sup_{t\in(t_{0}-\tau,t_{0}+\tau)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{r,\tau}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{\tau}\;dx\leq 2\sup_{t\in(t_{0}-\tau,t_{0}+\tau)}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{r}(x_{0})}\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|^{2}}{\tau}\;dx.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
), and let
\eta\in C_{0}^{\infty}(B_{R}(x_{0}))
be a function such that

\eta\geq 0,\quad\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{R}(x_{0})}\eta\;dx=1\quad\text{and}\quad\|\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}+R\|D\eta\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq c(n).

Then for
U_{R,S}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}
, there exists a constant
c=c(n,L)
such that

\displaystyle\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}\in(t_{0}-S,t_{0}+S)}|(u\eta)_{x_{0};R}(t_{2})-(u\eta)_{x_{0};R}(t_{1})|\leq c\frac{S}{R}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}[\widetilde{H}(z,|Du|)+\widetilde{H}(z,|F|)]\;dz,

where
\widetilde{H}:\Omega_{T}\times\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{+}
is denoted by
\widetilde{H}(z,\kappa)=a(z)\kappa^{p-1}+b(z)\kappa^{q-1}
for
z\in\Omega_{T}
and
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
.

Proof.

The proof can be completed following
[
22
, Lemma 2.4]
.
∎

The next lemma presents a parabolic Poincaré inequality.

Lemma 2.6
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
). Then for
U_{R,S}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}
,
m\in(1,q]
and
\theta\in(1/m,1]
, there exists a constant
c
depending on
n
,
N
,
m
and
L
such that

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}

\displaystyle\frac{|u-(u)_{U_{R,S}(z_{0})}|^{\theta m}}{R^{\theta m}}\,dz

(2.8)

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}|Du|^{\theta m}\,dz+c\left(\frac{S}{R^{2}}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptU_{R,S}(z_{0})}}[\widetilde{H}(z,|Du|)+\widetilde{H}(z,|F|)]\,dz\right)^{\theta m}.

Proof.

In the same way as
[
22
,
Lemma
2.5]
, the inequality (
2.8
) can be derived.
∎

3.
Reverse Hölder inequalities

We prove reverse Hölder type inequalities by dividing it into three cases, namely,
p
-intrinsic case,
q
-intrinsic case and
(p,q)
-intrinsic case.
The constant
K=K(n,p,q,\alpha,\delta,\omega_{a},[b]_{\alpha},M)>1
that appears below will be determined later. Moreover, throughout this section we consider radii
\rho\in(0,\rho_{0})
of the
p
-,
q
-, and
(p,q)
-intrinsic cylinders, where given any number
\eta\in(0,1)
, we choose
\rho_{0}>0
sufficiently small such that
\omega_{a}(\rho)\leq\eta
and
[b]_{\alpha}\,\rho^{\alpha}\leq\eta
for every
\rho\in(0,\rho_{0})
.

3.1.
Reverse Hölder inequality for
p
-phase

In this subsection, we prove a reverse Hölder inequality for the
p
-phase. In the
p
-intrinsic case, we consider the following assumptions:

(3.1)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &Ka(z_{0})\lambda^{p}\geq b(z_{0})\lambda^{q},\\
&a(z_{0})\geq\frac{\delta}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{a(z)}{3}\leq a(z_{0})\leq 3a(z)\ \,\text{for all}\,\,\,z\in Q_{\rho,\lambda}(z_{0}),\\
&{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}[H(z,|Du|)+H(z,|F|)]\;dz<a(z_{0})\lambda^{p}\ \,\text{ for every }\tau\in(\rho,c_{v}(2\rho)],\\
&{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}[H(z,|Du|)+H(z,|F|)]\;dz=a(z_{0})\lambda^{p}.\end{aligned}\right.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
). Then there exists a constant
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})\geq 1
such that for any
Q_{4\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}
with (
3.1
),
\tau\in[2\rho,4\rho]
and
\theta\in\left(\frac{q-1}{p},1\right]
,

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}[\widetilde{H}(z,|Du|)+\widetilde{H}(z,|F|)]\;dz

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in Q_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}a(\tilde{z})(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz

\displaystyle\quad+c\lambda^{-1+\frac{p}{q}}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz

\displaystyle\quad+c\lambda^{\frac{\alpha p}{n+2}}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta p}\;dz\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta}\left(\frac{p-1}{p}-\frac{\alpha}{n+2}\right)}.

Proof.

We note that
q-1<p
. Since
b(\cdot)\in C^{\alpha,\frac{\alpha}{2}}(\Omega_{T})
, we have

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}[\widetilde{H}(z,|Du|)+\widetilde{H}(z,|F|)]\;dz

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in Q_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}a(\tilde{z})\,(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz+c\omega_{a}(\tau){{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}[|Du|^{p-1}+|F|^{p-1}]\;dz

\displaystyle\quad+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in Q_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}b(\tilde{z})\,(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz+c\tau^{\alpha}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}[|Du|^{q-1}+|F|^{q-1}]\;dz

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in Q_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}a(\tilde{z})\,(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz+c(\delta)\omega_{a}(\tau){{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}[\widetilde{H}(z,|Du|)+\widetilde{H}(z,|F|)]\;dz

\displaystyle\quad+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in Q_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}b(\tilde{z})\,(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz+c\tau^{\alpha}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}[|Du|^{q-1}+|F|^{q-1}]\;dz

for some constant
c\geq 1
depending on
p
,
q
and
[b]_{\alpha}
.
We consider
\tau>0
so small that
\displaystyle c(\delta)\omega_{a}(\tau)\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Then we have

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}[\widetilde{H}(z,|Du|)+\widetilde{H}(z,|F|)]\;dz

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in Q_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}a(\tilde{z})\,(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in Q_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}b(\tilde{z})\,(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz

\displaystyle\quad+c\tau^{\alpha}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}(|Du|^{q-1}+|F|^{q-1})\;dz.

We estimate the second term on the right-hand side as follows:

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in Q_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}b(\tilde{z})(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz

\displaystyle\qquad\leq a(z_{0})^{\frac{1}{q}}K^{\frac{1}{q}}\lambda^{\frac{p}{q}-1}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in Q_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}b(\tilde{z})^{\frac{q-1}{q}}(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz

\displaystyle\qquad\leq a(z_{0})^{\frac{1}{q}}K^{\frac{1}{q}}\lambda^{\frac{p}{q}-1}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz.

Note that
|Q_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})|=c(n)\tau^{n+2}\frac{\lambda^{2-p}}{a(z_{0})}
. Then it follows from Hölder inequality that

\displaystyle\tau^{\alpha}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{q-1}\;dz

\displaystyle\;\leq\tau^{\alpha}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{\theta p}\;dz\right)^{\frac{q-1}{\theta p}}

\displaystyle\;=\tau^{\alpha}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{\theta p}\;dz\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta}\frac{\gamma}{p}}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{\theta p}\;dz\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta}\frac{q-1-\gamma}{p}}

\displaystyle\;\leq c\tau^{\alpha-\frac{(n+2)\gamma}{p}}\lambda^{\frac{(p-2)\gamma}{p}}\left({\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int_{Q_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{p}\;dz\right)^{\frac{\gamma}{p}}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{\theta p}\;dz\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta}\frac{q-1-\gamma}{p}}.

Now choosing
0<\gamma=\frac{\alpha p}{n+2}<p-1,
and using the second and third conditions of (
3.1
), we get

\displaystyle\tau^{\alpha}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{q-1}\;dz

\displaystyle\leq ca(z_{0})^{\frac{q-p}{p}}\lambda^{\frac{\alpha p}{n+2}}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{\theta p}\;dz\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta}\frac{p-1-\gamma}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq c\lambda^{\frac{\alpha p}{n+2}}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{\theta p}\;dz\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta}\frac{p-1-\gamma}{p}}.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
). Then there exists a constant
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})\geq 1
such that for any
Q_{4\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}
with (
3.1
),
\tau\in[2\rho,4\rho]
and
\theta\in(\frac{q-1}{p},1]
,

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\tau,\lambda}|^{\theta p}}{\tau^{\theta p}}\,dz

\displaystyle\quad\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|Du|)^{\theta}\,dz+c\lambda^{\left(2-p+\frac{\alpha p}{n+2}\right)\theta p}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta p}\,dz\right)^{p-1-\frac{\alpha p}{n+2}}

\displaystyle\qquad+c\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|F|)\,dz\right)^{\theta}.

Proof.

We first observe from the second condition of (
3.1
) that

(3.2)

\frac{\delta}{6}\leq a(z)\leq\left|\hskip-0.85358pt\left|a\right|\hskip-0.85358pt\right|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})},\quad\forall z\in Q_{4\rho,\lambda}(z_{0}).

By
Lemma

2.6
and
Lemma

3.1
, there exists a constant
c
depending on
data
such that

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\tau,\lambda}|^{\theta p}}{\tau^{\theta p}}\;dz

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}|Du|^{\theta p}\;dz+c\underbrace{\left(\lambda^{2-p}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz\right)^{\theta p}}_{\mathrm{I}}

\displaystyle\quad+c\underbrace{\left(\lambda^{1-p+\frac{p}{q}}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz\right)^{\theta p}}_{\mathrm{II}}

\displaystyle\quad+c\lambda^{(2-p+\frac{\alpha p}{n+2})\theta p}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta p}\;dz\right)^{p-1-\frac{\alpha p}{n+2}}.

Estimate of
\mathrm{I}
:

Using Hölder’s inequality, the second condition of (
3.1
) and (
3.2
), we estimate

\displaystyle\lambda^{(2-p)\theta p}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz\right)^{\theta p}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 3^{p-1}\frac{\lambda^{(2-p)\theta p}}{a(z_{0})^{\theta(p-2)}}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}a(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta p}\;dz\right)^{p-2}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\frac{a^{\theta}(z)}{a^{\theta}(z_{0})}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta p}\,dz

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 3^{p-1}\left(\frac{2}{\delta}\right)^{\theta}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}a(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta p}\;dz\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(H_{1}(z,|Du|)+H_{1}(z,|F|)\right)^{\theta}\,dz.

Estimate of
\mathrm{II}
:
Similarly, using Hölder’s inequality and the third condition of (
3.1
), we get

\displaystyle\lambda^{\left(1-p+\frac{p}{q}\right)\theta p}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz\right)^{\theta p}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{\left(1-p+\frac{p}{q}\right)\theta p}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta q}\;dz\right)^{\frac{p(q-1)}{q}}

\displaystyle=\lambda^{\left(1-p+\frac{p}{q}\right)\theta p}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta q}\;dz\right)^{\frac{p(q-1)-q}{q}}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta q}\;dz

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq: p-phase condition}}{\leq}\lambda^{\left(1-p+\frac{p}{q}\right)\theta p}\lambda^{-\left(1-p+\frac{p}{q}\right)\theta p}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta q}\;dz={{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta q}\;dz,

where we have used

\displaystyle\frac{p+q}{pq}=\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}\leq 1\implies p+q\leq pq\implies q\leq p(q-1)

since
2\leq p\leq q
.
Finally, using Hölder inequality, we get the desired estimate.
∎

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
). Then there exists a constant
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})\geq 1
such that for any
Q_{4\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}
with (
3.1
),
\tau\in[2\rho,4\rho]
and
\theta\in((q-1)/p,1]
,

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in Q_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}b(\tilde{z})^{\theta}\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\tau,\lambda}|^{\theta q}}{\tau^{\theta q}}\;dz

\displaystyle\quad\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}(H(z,|Du|))^{\theta}\;dz+c\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|F|)\;dz\right)^{\theta}

\displaystyle\qquad+c\lambda^{\left(2-p+\frac{\alpha p}{n+2}\right)\theta p}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta p}\;dz\right)^{p-1-\frac{\alpha p}{n+2}}.

Proof.

The proof follows from
[
22
, Lemma 3.3]
.
∎

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
). Then for
Q_{c_{v}(2\rho),\lambda}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}
with (
3.1
), there exists a constant
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})\geq 1
such that

S(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}=\sup_{I_{2\rho,\lambda}(t_{0})}\mathchoice{{\vbox{\hbox{$\textstyle-$}}\kern-4.86108pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptstyle-$}}\kern-3.43057pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.908pt}}{{\vbox{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle-$}}\kern-2.76045pt}}\!\int_{B_{2\rho}(x_{0})}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{2}}{(2\rho)^{2}}\;dx\leq c\lambda^{2}.

Proof.

Let
2\rho\leq\rho_{1}<\rho_{2}\leq 4\rho
. By
Lemma

2.4
, there exists a constant
c
depending on
n,p,q,\nu
and
L
such that

\displaystyle\lambda^{p-2}S(u)_{z_{0};\rho_{1},\lambda}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{c\rho_{2}^{q}}{(\rho_{2}-\rho_{1})^{q}}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho_{2},\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(a(z)\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\rho_{2},\lambda}|^{p}}{\rho^{p}_{2}}+b(z)\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\rho_{2},\lambda}|^{q}}{\rho^{q}_{2}}\right)dz

(3.3)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{c\rho_{2}^{2}\lambda^{p-2}}{(\rho_{2}-\rho_{1})^{2}}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho_{2},\lambda}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\rho_{2},\lambda}|^{2}}{\rho_{2}^{2}}\;dz+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho_{2},\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|F|)\;dz.

We note that the term involving
a(z)
in the right-hand side can be estimated by using
Lemma

3.2
with
\theta=1,

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho_{2},\lambda}(z_{0})}}a(z)\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\rho_{2},\lambda}|^{p}}{\rho^{p}_{2}}\,dz\leq||a||_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho_{2},\lambda}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\rho_{2},\lambda}|^{p}}{\rho^{p}_{2}}\,dz\leq c||a||_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})}\lambda^{p}.

The rest of the proof follows from
[
22
, Lemma 4.3]
. Indeed, using the Hölder regularity of
b(\cdot),
we have

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho_{2},\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\rho_{2},\lambda}|^{q}}{\rho^{q}_{2}}\;dz

\displaystyle\quad\leq\underbrace{{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho_{2},\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in Q_{\rho_{2},\lambda}(z_{0})}b(\tilde{z})\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\rho_{2},\lambda}|^{q}}{\rho^{q}_{2}}\,dz}_{\mathrm{I}}+\underbrace{[b]_{\alpha}\rho^{\alpha}_{2}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho_{2},\lambda}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\rho_{2},\lambda}|^{q}}{\rho^{q}_{2}}\,dz}_{\mathrm{II}}.

Estimate of
\mathrm{I}
:
Directly using
Lemma

3.3
with
\theta=1
and the second condition of (
3.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho_{2},\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in Q_{\rho_{2},\lambda}(z_{0})}b(\tilde{z})\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\rho_{2},\lambda}|^{q}}{\rho^{q}_{2}}\,dz\leq c\lambda^{p}.

Estimate of
\mathrm{II}
:
Using
Lemma

2.1
, we estimate

\displaystyle[b]_{\alpha}\rho^{\alpha}_{2}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho_{2},\lambda}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\rho_{2},\lambda}|^{q}}{\rho^{q}_{2}}\,dz\leq c\lambda^{p}.

Moreover, following
[
22
, Lemma 4.3]
, we estimate the second term of (
3.3
) as follows:

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho_{2},\lambda}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0};\rho_{2},\lambda}|^{2}}{\rho_{2}^{2}}\;dz\leq c\lambda\left(S(u)_{z_{0},\rho_{2},\lambda}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Combining all the estimates, using the third condition of (
3.1
) and Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle S(u)_{z_{0},\rho_{1},\lambda}\leq\frac{1}{2}S(u)_{z_{0},\rho_{2},\lambda}+c\left[\frac{\rho^{q}_{2}}{(\rho_{2}-\rho_{1})^{q}}+\frac{\rho^{4}_{2}}{(\rho_{2}-\rho_{1})^{4}}\right]\lambda^{2}.

Finally, applying
Lemma

2.2
, we conclude the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.5
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
). Then for
Q_{c_{v}(2\rho),\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)\subset\Omega_{T}
with (
3.1
), there exist constants
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})\geq 1
and
\theta_{0}=\theta_{0}(n,p,q)\in(0,1)
such that for any
\theta\in\left(\theta_{0},1\right)
,

\displaystyle{{{\displaystyle\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}H\left(z,\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|}{2\rho}\right)dz

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\left(\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}a(\tilde{z})^{\theta}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{\theta p}}{(2\rho)^{\theta p}}+\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}a(\tilde{z})^{\theta}|Du|^{\theta p}\right)dz\;\left(S(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right)^{\frac{(1-\theta)p}{2}}

\displaystyle+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\left(\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}b(\tilde{z})^{\theta}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{\theta q}}{(2\rho)^{\theta q}}+\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}b(\tilde{z})^{\theta}|Du|^{\theta q}\right)dz

\displaystyle\times\lambda^{(p-q)(1-\theta)}\;\left(S(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right)^{\frac{(1-\theta)q}{2}}.

Proof.

Using the uniform continuity of
a(\cdot)
and Hölder continuity of
b(\cdot),
we have

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}H\left(z,\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|}{2\rho}\right)dz

\displaystyle\leq\omega_{a}(2\rho){{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{p}}{(2\rho)^{p}}\,dz+{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}a(\tilde{z})\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{p}}{(2\rho)^{p}}\,dz

\displaystyle\quad+{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}b(\tilde{z})\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{q}}{(2\rho)^{q}}\,dz+[b]_{\alpha}(2\rho)^{\alpha}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{q}}{(2\rho)^{q}}\,dz

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq: p-phase condition}}{\leq}c(\delta)\omega_{a}(2\rho){{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}H\left(z,\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|}{2\rho}\right)dz+{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}a(\tilde{z})\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{p}}{(2\rho)^{p}}\,dz

\displaystyle\quad+{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}b(\tilde{z})\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{q}}{(2\rho)^{q}}\,dz+[b]_{\alpha}(2\rho)^{\alpha}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{q}}{(2\rho)^{q}}\,dz.

We choose
\rho>0
so small that
c(\delta)\omega_{a}(2\rho)\leq\frac{1}{2}
.
Then it follows that

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}H\left(z,\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|}{2\rho}\right)dz

\displaystyle\leq c\underbrace{{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}a(\tilde{z})\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{p}}{(2\rho)^{p}}\,dz}_{\mathrm{I}}

\displaystyle\quad+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}b(\tilde{z})\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{q}}{(2\rho)^{q}}\,dz

\displaystyle\quad+c[b]_{\alpha}(2\rho)^{\alpha}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{q}}{(2\rho)^{q}}\,dz.

Estimate of
\mathrm{I}
:
Choosing
\sigma=p,s=\theta p,r=2,\vartheta=\theta,
in
Lemma

2.1
, we estimate

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}a(\tilde{z})\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{p}}{(2\rho)^{p}}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\left(\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}a(\tilde{z})^{\theta}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{\theta p}}{(2\rho)^{\theta p}}+\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}a(\tilde{z})^{\theta}|Du|^{\theta p}\right)dz

\displaystyle\quad\times\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}a(\tilde{z})^{1-\theta}\left(S(u)_{z_{0},2\rho,\lambda}\right)^{\frac{(1-\theta)p}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\left(\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}a(\tilde{z})^{\theta}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{\theta p}}{(2\rho)^{\theta p}}+\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}a(\tilde{z})^{\theta}|Du|^{\theta p}\right)dz\left(S(u)_{z_{0},2\rho,\lambda}\right)^{\frac{(1-\theta)p}{2}},

where we have used
\displaystyle{\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}a(\tilde{z})^{1-\theta}\leq\max\left\{1,||a||^{1-\theta_{0}}_{L^{\infty}(\Omega_{T})}\right\}.
Now following
[
22
, Lemma 4.4]
, we have

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}b(\tilde{z})\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{q}}{(2\rho)^{q}}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\left(\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}b(\tilde{z})^{\theta}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{\theta q}}{(2\rho)^{\theta q}}+\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}b(\tilde{z})^{\theta}|Du|^{\theta q}\right)dz\,\lambda^{(p-q)(1-\theta)}\left(S(u)_{z_{0},2\rho,\lambda}\right)^{\frac{(1-\theta)p}{2}}

and

\displaystyle(2\rho)^{\alpha}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{q}}{(2\rho)^{q}}\,dz

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}}\left(\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}a(\tilde{z})^{\theta}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{\theta p}}{(2\rho)^{\theta p}}+\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}a(\tilde{z})^{\theta}|Du|^{\theta p}\right)dz\left(S(u)_{z_{0},2\rho,\lambda}\right)^{\frac{(1-\theta)p}{2}},

where we also used the second condition of (
3.1
). Combining the estimates above proves the lemma.
∎

Now we are ready to prove the reverse Hölder inequality for
p
-phase.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
). Then for
Q_{c_{v}(2\rho),\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)\subset\Omega_{T}
with (
3.1
), there exist constants
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})\geq 1
and
\theta_{0}=\theta_{0}(n,p,q)\in(0,1)
such that for any
\theta\in(\theta_{0},1),

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|Du|)\;dz\leq c\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}[H(z,|Du|)]^{\theta}\;dz\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta}}+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|F|)\;dz.

Proof.

From the energy estimate,
Lemma

2.4
, we have

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|Du|)\;dz

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H\left(z,\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0},2\rho,\lambda}|}{2\rho}\right)dz

\displaystyle\quad+c\lambda^{p-2}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0},2\rho,\lambda}|^{2}}{(2\rho)^{2}}\;dz+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|F|)\;dz.

Now applying
Lemma

3.4
and
Lemma

3.5
, we estimate the right-hand side of the above inequality as follows:

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H\left(z,\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0},2\rho,\lambda}|}{2\rho}\right)dz\leq c\lambda^{(1-\theta)p}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|Du|)^{\theta}\,dz

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\lambda^{(1-\theta)p}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}a(\tilde{z})^{\theta}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{\theta p}}{(2\rho)^{\theta p}}+\inf_{Q_{2\rho,\lambda}\left(z_{0}\right)}b(\tilde{z})^{\theta}\frac{\left|u-(u)_{z_{0};2\rho,\lambda}\right|^{\theta q}}{(2\rho)^{\theta q}}\right)dz.

The rest of the proof follows from
[
22
, Lemma 4.5]
by applying
Lemma

3.2
,
Lemma

3.3
, Young’s inequality and the fourth condition of (
3.1
). For the sake of completeness, we include it here.

Indeed, by
Lemma

3.2
and
Lemma

3.3
, we have

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H\left(z,\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0},2\rho,\lambda}|}{2\rho}\right)dz

(3.4)

\displaystyle\quad\leq c\lambda^{p(1-\beta\theta)}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|Du|)^{\theta}\,dz\right)^{\beta}+c\lambda^{p(1-\beta\theta)}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|F|)\,dz\right)^{\theta\beta}

and

\displaystyle\lambda^{p-2}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0},2\rho,\lambda}|^{2}}{(2\rho)^{2}}\;dz

\displaystyle\leq c\lambda^{p-\beta}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|Du|)^{\theta}\,dz\right)^{\frac{\beta}{\theta p}}

(3.5)

\displaystyle\quad+c\lambda^{p-\beta}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|F|)\,dz\right)^{\frac{\beta}{p}}

for

\beta:=\min\left\{p-1-\frac{\alpha p}{n+2},\frac{1}{2}\right\}.

Now applying Young’s inequality in (
3.4
) with
\left(\frac{1}{1-\beta\theta},\frac{1}{\beta\theta}\right)
and in (
3.5
) with
\left(\frac{p}{p-\beta},\frac{p}{\beta}\right)
, we get

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H\left(z,\frac{|u-(u)_{z_{0},2\rho,\lambda}|}{2\rho}\right)dz

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\varepsilon\lambda^{p}+c(\varepsilon)\left[\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|Du|)^{\theta}\,dz\right)^{\frac{1}{\theta}}+{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{2\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|F|)\,dz\right].

Finally, using the fourth condition in (
3.1
), we obtain the desired estimate.
∎

3.2.
Reverse Hölder inequality for
q
-phase

In this subsection, we prove reverse Hölder inequality for
q
-phase. In the
q
-intrinsic case, we consider the following assumptions:

(3.6)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &Ka(z_{0})\lambda^{p}\leq b(z_{0})\lambda^{q},\\
&b(z_{0})\geq\frac{\delta}{2}\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{b(z)}{3}\leq b(z_{0})\leq 3b(z)\ \,\text{for all}\,\,\,z\in J_{\rho,\lambda}(z_{0}),\\
&{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left[H(z,|Du|)+H(z,|F|)\right]dz<b(z_{0})\lambda^{q}\ \,\text{for every }\tau\in(\rho,c_{v}(2\rho)],\\
&{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left[H(z,|Du|)+H(z,|F|)\right]dz=b(z_{0})\lambda^{q}.\end{aligned}\right.

We note that, from the second and third conditions above, we have

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z_{0})\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{q}\,dz\leq{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}3b(z)\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{q}\,dz<c\,b(z_{0})\lambda^{q}.

This implies that

(3.7)

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{q}\,dz\leq c\lambda^{q}.

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
). Then there exists a constant
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})\geq 1
such that for any
J_{4\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}
with (
3.6
),
\tau\in[2\rho,4\rho]
and
\theta\in(q-1/p,1]
,

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left[\widetilde{H}(z,|Du|)+\widetilde{H}(z,|F|)\right]dz

\displaystyle\quad\leq c\lambda^{-1+\frac{q}{p}}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}a(z)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz

\displaystyle\qquad+c\omega_{a}(\tau){{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz.

Proof.

First, using the uniform continuity of
a(\cdot)
and Hölder continuity of
b(\cdot),
we observe that

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left[\widetilde{H}(z,|Du|)+\widetilde{H}(z,|F|)\right]dz

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in J_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}a(\tilde{z})\,(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz+c\omega_{a}(\tau){{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|^{p-1}+|F|^{p-1}\right)dz

\displaystyle\quad+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in J_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}b(\tilde{z})\,(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz+c\tau^{\alpha}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|^{q-1}+|F|^{q-1}\right)dz

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptQ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in J_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}a(\tilde{z})\,(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz+c\omega_{a}(\tau){{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|^{p-1}+|F|^{p-1}\right)dz

\displaystyle\quad+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in J_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}b(\tilde{z})\,(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz+c(\delta)\tau^{\alpha}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left[\widetilde{H}(z,|Du|)+\widetilde{H}(z,|F|)\right]dz

for some constant
c\geq 1
depending on
p
,
q
,
[b]_{\alpha}
and
\delta
.
We consider
\tau>0
so small that
\displaystyle c(\delta)\tau^{\alpha}\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left[\widetilde{H}(z,|Du|)+\widetilde{H}(z,|F|)\right]dz

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in J_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}a(\tilde{z})\,(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\inf_{\tilde{z}\in J_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}b(\tilde{z})\,(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz

\displaystyle\quad+c\omega_{a}(\tau){{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|^{p-1}+|F|^{p-1}\right)dz.

Using the first condition of (
3.6
), we get

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left[\widetilde{H}(z,|Du|)+\widetilde{H}(z,|F|)\right]dz

\displaystyle\leq K^{-\frac{1}{p}}b(z_{0})^{\frac{1}{p}}\lambda^{-1+\frac{q}{p}}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}a(z)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}\,(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz+c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)\,(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz

\displaystyle\quad+c\omega_{a}(\tau){{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{p-1}dz.

This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.8
.

Let
u
be a weak solution to (
1.1
). Then for
J_{4\rho,\lambda}(z_{0})\subset\Omega_{T}
with (
3.6
),
\tau\in[2\rho,4\rho]
and
\theta\in(\frac{q-1}{p},1]
, there exists a constant
c=c(\textnormal{{data}})\geq 1
such that

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)^{q}_{z_{0};\tau,\lambda}|^{\theta q}}{\tau^{\theta q}}\,dz\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|Du|)^{\theta}\,dz+c\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}H(z,|F|)\,dz\right)^{\theta}.

Proof.

From
2.8
of
Lemma

2.6
, we have

\displaystyle{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}\frac{|u-(u)^{q}_{z_{0};\tau,\lambda}|^{\theta q}}{\tau^{\theta q}}\;dz

\displaystyle\leq c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}|Du|^{\theta q}\;dz+c\underbrace{\left(\lambda^{2-q}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz\right)^{\theta q}}_{\mathrm{I}}

\displaystyle\quad+c\underbrace{\left(\lambda^{1-q+\frac{q}{p}}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}a(z)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz\right)^{\theta q}}_{\mathrm{II}}

\displaystyle\quad+c\underbrace{\lambda^{(2-q)\theta q}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz\right)^{\theta q}}_{\mathrm{III}}.

We estimate
\mathrm{I},\mathrm{II},\mathrm{III}
as follows.

Estimate of
\mathrm{I}
:
Using the second condition of (
3.6
), that is
\frac{b(z)}{3}\leq b(z_{0})\leq 3b(z),
and Hölder’s inequality, we get

\displaystyle\left(\lambda^{2-q}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)(|Du|+|F|)^{q-1}\;dz\right)^{\theta q}

\displaystyle\leq c\lambda^{(2-q)\theta q}b(z_{0})^{\theta}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)^{\frac{q-1}{q}}\left(|Du|+|F|\right)^{q-1}\right)^{\theta q}

\displaystyle\leq c\lambda^{(2-q)\theta q}b(z_{0})^{\theta}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta q}\,dz\right)^{q-1}

\displaystyle\leq c\lambda^{(2-q)\theta q}b(z_{0})^{\theta}b(z_{0})^{\theta(q-2)}\lambda^{\theta q(q-2)}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta q}\,dz.

Estimate of
\mathrm{II}
:
Since
q(p-1)-p\geq 0,
using Hölder’s inequality and the third condition of (
3.6
), we get

\displaystyle\left(\lambda^{1-q+\frac{q}{p}}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}a(z)^{\frac{p-1}{p}}(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz\right)^{\theta q}

\displaystyle\leq c\lambda^{(1-q+\frac{q}{p})\theta q}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}a(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta p}\;dz\right)^{\frac{q(p-1)}{p}}

\displaystyle=c\lambda^{(1-q+\frac{q}{p})\theta q}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}a(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta p}\;dz\right)^{\frac{q(p-1)-p}{p}}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}a(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta p}\;dz

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq: q-phase condition}}{\leq}c\lambda^{\left(\frac{q(1-p)}{p}+\frac{q(p-1)}{p}\right)\theta q}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}a(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta p}\;dz=c{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}a(z)^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta p}\;dz.

Estimate of
\mathrm{III}
:
Here, we use the second and third conditions of (
3.6
). Indeed,

\displaystyle\lambda^{(2-q)\theta q}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}(|Du|+|F|)^{p-1}\;dz\right)^{\theta q}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda^{(2-q)\theta q}\left({{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta q}\;dz\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq: q-phase condition}}{\leq}\frac{\lambda^{(p-q)\theta q}}{b(z_{0})^{\theta}}{{{\int\hskip-7.96674pt\int}\hskip-15.93347pt-\hskip-4.26773pt-}_{\hskip-3.69899ptJ_{\tau,\lambda}(z_{0})}}b(z_{0})^{\theta}(|Du|+|F|)^{\theta q}\,dz
